> > > > > > > AR-37: I see the change, but am not sure how that will evaluate. Your solution solved the oddities of the text juxtaposition, but I think there may still be an underlying accessibility issue in that the label doesn’t always describe the button text accurately. This was initially flagged by BeAccessible because the button text wasn’t descriptive enough for users who are tabbing through to understand the context. With the new change, the button that says “Sign Up” would present as "Learn More About CPR Class September 23rd 10am-2pm $100" to a screen reader. Those sound like two different actions, so I have a feeling it won’t pass validation.  Since the folks at Aspen can control that button text, I think this may just need to fall on their shoulders to make those accessible with logical text rather than try to create a programmatic solution that may not always fit. If we go that route, the aria label wouldnt’ even be needed if I understand the spec correctly. Thoughts?

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >

Subject: Re: Accordions

Thanks for letting me know the out of office dates. It's good to get that
R&R.

*AR-37*: I've narrowed this down to two files that affect these areas. It
sounds feasible, but the button text seems to be what was giving those
unpredictable values, so I'll work with it and see where it goes.

*AR-17 to 21*: the variables Hybrid added for these are unclear of where
the data flows from so now I know they don't come from the CMS. Yes I think
this is feasible, I'll have to work with the twig files to add CMS fields
for images then change the alt text on all instances of images within Vue
to use those variables. Very possible, just time consuming.

*About page hover gradient*: I searched the code and this is the only place
that this combination (hover:bg-gradient-to-r hover:from-secondary-500
hover:via-secondary-600 hover:to-secondary-500) of gradient is used.

Using the Dark Gradient on the homepage buttons:hover (from: secondary-600,
via: secondary-700, to: quaternary-800), secondary-600 and quaternary-800
fail contrast with white text so I'm looking at using [ from:
secondary-700, via: secondary-800, to: quaternary-900 ]. This passes
contrast across the gradient. Looks like this:
[image: Screenshot 2024-03-15 at 11.26.05 AM.png]
Let me know if you want to change that or if it's good.

*Light green gradient*: I updated this gradient for the navigation in
navigation.pcss

*Mailchimp*: I checked into updating the embed code
<https://mailchimp.com/help/add-a-signup-form-to-your-website/>, but it
looks like you'll have to make the customizations and send me the embed
code that it generates. If you can put it in an html file that would help,
if not no biggie.

>>>>>>>>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>>>>>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>>>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>>>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>>>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> data() {
>>>>>>>>>> return {
>>>>>>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>>>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>>>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>> watch: {
>>>>>>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>>>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>> methods: {
>>>>>>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>>>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>>>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>>>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>>>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])'
>>>>>>>>>> );
>>>>>>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>>>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>>>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>>>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much
>>>>>>>>>> time into it.
>>>>>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> Shaun,
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the
>>>>>>>>>>>>> docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for
>>>>>>>>>>>>> instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's
>>>>>>>>>>>>> required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without
>>>>>>>>>>>>> additional coding.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer
>>>>>>>>>>>>> that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems
>>>>>>>>>>>>> odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that
>>>>>>>>>>>>> "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component,
>>>>>>>>>>>>> such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim
>>>>>>>>>>>>> possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains
>>>>>>>>>>>>> questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on
>>>>>>>>>>>>> next.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but
>>>>>>>>>>>>> we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is
>>>>>>>>>>>>> open and we should be good.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case,
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> it's not working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>

==== EMAIL 503 ====
Date: Fri, 15 Mar 2024 12:12:03 -0600
From: Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
To:   Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
Subject: Re: Accordions

Hi Eric,

I’ll second the busy week sentiment! Thanks for continuing to make time for this.

FYI, I’ll be away from home Saturday - Tuesday to spend a little spring break time with the family. I’ll try to check emails nightly if there’s anything urgent.

AR-37: Sorry for the confusion on this. It’s tough to find ways that work universally in this context. We can’t have the buttons getting really long with lots of text since that poses an issue for sighted users on mobile. On the flip side, the button needs to be easily understood by someone listening to audio-only cues. I thought of what I hope is a better solution: For the aria label, what do you think of pulling in the item title (i.e. "Job Opportunities at Aspen Rec”) then appending a colon, and pulling in the button text (i.e. “Apply Today!”). That should lead to a final output like: "Job Opportunities at Aspen Rec: Apply Today!”  Can you think of any cases where that would lead to confusing text being read by screen readers for people who can’t see the button?

AR 17-21: These items will take a little more work, unfortunately. Hybrid didn’t build in a way for the folks at Aspen to enter alt text for images. They either seemed to have ignored the field altogether, or chose to populate values automatically using either the file name, or text related to the image if in a slider or other “limited” context. To bring this into compliance, we’ll need to add the ability for Aspen to specify alt text for all images on the site. Ideally, I’m thinking this would be done whenever they upload the image and could be managed in the asset library so that the value doesn’t have to be entered separately for each use of the image if it’s used in multiple places across the site. How feasible is that approach? Feel free to call if you’d like to discuss, as I know this one will probably pose some complications….

Shaun Cooney } MONUMENT GRAPHICS | DESIGN + WEB
PO Box 299, Fruita, CO 81521

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable

>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> data() {
>>>>>>>>> return {
>>>>>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>> watch: {
>>>>>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>> methods: {
>>>>>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])'
>>>>>>>>> );
>>>>>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much
>>>>>>>>> time into it.
>>>>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>>>>> On Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <
>>>>>>>>>>>> eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> Shaun,
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the
>>>>>>>>>>>> docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for
>>>>>>>>>>>> instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's
>>>>>>>>>>>> required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without
>>>>>>>>>>>> additional coding.
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's
>>>>>>>>>>>> used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and
>>>>>>>>>>>> we should be good.
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.
>>>>>>>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case,
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> it's not working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>

> AR-37: I see the change, but am not sure how that will evaluate. Your
> solution solved the oddities of the text juxtaposition, but I think there
> may still be an underlying accessibility issue in that the label doesn’t
> always describe the button text accurately. This was initially flagged by
> BeAccessible because the button text wasn’t descriptive enough for users
> who are tabbing through to understand the context. With the new change, the
> button that says “Sign Up” would present as "Learn More About CPR Class
> September 23rd 10am-2pm $100" to a screen reader. Those sound like two
> different actions, so I have a feeling it won’t pass validation.  Since the
> folks at Aspen can control that button text, I think this may just need to
> fall on their shoulders to make those accessible with logical text rather
> than try to create a programmatic solution that may not always fit. If we
> go that route, the aria label wouldnt’ even be needed if I understand the
> spec correctly. Thoughts?
>

>>>>>>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>>>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> data() {
>>>>>>>> return {
>>>>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>> watch: {
>>>>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>> methods: {
>>>>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>>>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much
>>>>>>>> time into it.
>>>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs
>>>>>>>>>>> on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance,
>>>>>>>>>>> when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to
>>>>>>>>>>> use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional
>>>>>>>>>>> coding.
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's
>>>>>>>>>>> used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and
>>>>>>>>>>> we should be good.

>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

Subject: Re: Accordions

Hi again,

AR-37: I see the change, but am not sure how that will evaluate. Your solution solved the oddities of the text juxtaposition, but I think there may still be an underlying accessibility issue in that the label doesn’t always describe the button text accurately. This was initially flagged by BeAccessible because the button text wasn’t descriptive enough for users who are tabbing through to understand the context. With the new change, the button that says “Sign Up” would present as "Learn More About CPR Class September 23rd 10am-2pm $100" to a screen reader. Those sound like two different actions, so I have a feeling it won’t pass validation.  Since the folks at Aspen can control that button text, I think this may just need to fall on their shoulders to make those accessible with logical text rather than try to create a programmatic solution that may not always fit. If we go that route, the aria label wouldnt’ even be needed if I understand the spec correctly. Thoughts?

Regarding Mailchimp, it looks like Mark had previously pasted MailChimp’s embed code verbatim on lines 45 - 93 of the footer.twig file. Since that code is at least 3 eyars old, I’d start by deleting those lines and replacing with the new code I attached previously. I’d prefer to use the new stuff in any event since it’ll have the latest improvements from MailChimp and may solve some of these accessibility concerns.

Thanks!

Shaun Cooney } MONUMENT GRAPHICS | DESIGN + WEB

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

>>>>>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>>
>>>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> data() {
>>>>>>> return {
>>>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>> watch: {
>>>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>> methods: {
>>>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>>>> },
>>>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>> }
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much
>>>>>>> time into it.
>>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs
>>>>>>>>>> on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance,
>>>>>>>>>> when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to
>>>>>>>>>> use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional
>>>>>>>>>> coding.
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's
>>>>>>>>>> used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and
>>>>>>>>>> we should be good.

>>>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem
>>>>>>>>>>>>> when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

>>>>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>
>>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>>
>>>>>> data() {
>>>>>> return {
>>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> },
>>>>>>
>>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> },
>>>>>> watch: {
>>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> },
>>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>>> },
>>>>>> methods: {
>>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>>> },
>>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> } else {
>>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> }
>>>>>> }
>>>>>>
>>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>>>>>> into it.
>>>>>>

>>>>>>>>> on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance,
>>>>>>>>> when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to
>>>>>>>>> use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional
>>>>>>>>> coding.

>>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:

>>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's
>>>>>>>>> used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and

>>>>>>>>>>>> For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates

>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem
>>>>>>>>>>>> when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

AR-2 Focus on page load does not go to modal (nor is it trapped there, but we’ve talked about that and I know you’re working on it). Mainly just noting this to help myself not lose track of it...

AR-5 My Apple screen reader reads the two words of the top-level navigation items as two separate words within a group. We may need to either style the top word and add a separator using a CSS pseudo element or just eliminate the bar altogether. What do you think? In the backend, I think there’s two lines for these items, so we may need to keep that structure, meaning the styled method makes more sense?

AR-8/12 - Looks good. I don’t see it flagged in the report, but the three gray nav links in the upper right don’t pass contrast. Can you please darken those? #6B7380 or anything reasonably close that you already have cooked up would work.

AR-34: See attached for what needs focus highlighted. Also applies to all the news items I believe. Also just wanted to make sure you have access to the screenshots & videos from BeAccessible that help reference those issues: https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1qpdSQR3pg4rAICPFLpkxGLaNzAL5yBQY?usp=sharing.

AR-37: Currently seeing “there’s a ’null’ value pulling in after the button for the aria label. If proving problematic, just let me know and I can hound home the point to Aspen that the button text needs to be self-explanatory, not generic.

Then for the Mailchimp form, I’m still seeing the old red and no autocomplete function. I see where you have the new red in the code, but no !important declaration, so maybe that’s just a sync thing. When contrast checking the form, it looks like we’ll also need to darken the subscribe button background. I didn’t see that in the report, so wanted to make sure we correct that as well.

Thanks!

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > }

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

>>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's
>>>>> basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript
>>>>> - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>>
>>>>> data() {
>>>>> return {
>>>>> showModal: false,
>>>>> }
>>>>> },
>>>>>
>>>>> mounted() {
>>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>> }
>>>>> },
>>>>> watch: {
>>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>> } else {
>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>> }
>>>>> }
>>>>> },
>>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>>> },
>>>>> methods: {
>>>>> closeModal() {
>>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>>> },
>>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.
>>>>> length - 1];
>>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>>> }
>>>>> } else {
>>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>>> }
>>>>> }
>>>>> }
>>>>> }
>>>>> }
>>>>>
>>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>>>>> into it.
>>>>>

>>>>>>>> Shaun,
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on
>>>>>>>> this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
>>>>>>>> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
>>>>>>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria-
>>>>>>>> implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For
>>>>>>>> instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling
>>>>>>>> for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this
>>>>>>>> isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to
>>>>>>>> be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing
>>>>>>>> something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is
>>>>>>>> "no aria is better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's
>>>>>>>> used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and
>>>>>>>> we should be good.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

>>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem
>>>>>>>>>>> when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the
>>>>>>>>>>>    modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> > > > > > > > > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > > > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into it.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.
