> > > > > > > > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > > > > > > return {
> > > > > > > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > > > > > > },
> > > > > > > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > > }
> > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into it.
> > > > > > > > > > >

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Shaun,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

>>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>>> wrote:
>>>
>>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically
>>>> what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the
>>>> strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>>> vue-project environment:
>>>>
>>>> data() {
>>>> return {
>>>> showModal: false,
>>>> }
>>>> },
>>>>
>>>> mounted() {
>>>> this.showModal = true;
>>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>> }
>>>> },
>>>> watch: {
>>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>>> if (newValue) {
>>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>> } else {
>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>> }
>>>> }
>>>> },
>>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>>> },
>>>> methods: {
>>>> closeModal() {
>>>> this.showModal = false;
>>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>>> },
>>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button,
>>>> [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length
>>>> - 1];
>>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>>> }
>>>> } else {
>>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>>> event.preventDefault();
>>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>>> }
>>>> }
>>>> }
>>>> }
>>>> }
>>>>
>>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>>>> into it.
>>>>

>>>>>>> Shaun,
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on
>>>>>>> this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
>>>>>>> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
>>>>>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the
>>>>>>> modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation
>>>>>>> and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance,
>>>>>>> there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the
>>>>>>> overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a
>>>>>>> tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be
>>>>>>> here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something
>>>>>>> but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is
>>>>>>> better than bad aria".
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used
>>>>>>> as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're
>>>>>>> pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and
>>>>>>> we should be good.
>>>>>>>
>>>>>>> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem
>>>>>>>>>> when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal
>>>>>>>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
>> wrote:
>>
>>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically
>>> what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the
>>> strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>>> vue-project environment:
>>>
>>> data() {
>>> return {
>>> showModal: false,
>>> }
>>> },
>>>
>>> mounted() {
>>> this.showModal = true;
>>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>> }
>>> },
>>> watch: {
>>> showModal(newValue) {
>>> if (newValue) {
>>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>> } else {
>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>> }
>>> }
>>> },
>>> beforeDestroy() {
>>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>>> },
>>> methods: {
>>> closeModal() {
>>> this.showModal = false;
>>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>>> },
>>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href],
>>> input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length
>>> - 1];
>>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>>> event.preventDefault();
>>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>>> }
>>> } else {
>>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>>> event.preventDefault();
>>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>>> }
>>> }
>>> }
>>> }
>>> }
>>>
>>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>>> into it.
>>>

>>>>>>
>>>>>> Shaun,
>>>>>>
>>>>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on
>>>>>> this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
>>>>>> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
>>>>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal,
>>>>>> which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>>>>
>>>>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be
>>>>>> adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by
>>>>>> half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to
>>>>>> sighted users.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation
>>>>>> and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance,
>>>>>> there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the
>>>>>> overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a
>>>>>> tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be
>>>>>> here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something
>>>>>> but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is
>>>>>> better than bad aria".
>>>>>>
>>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used
>>>>>> as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

>>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal
>>>>>>>>> based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic
>>>>>>>>> elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not
>>>>>>>>> working the way it should.
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem
>>>>>>>>> when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few
>>>>>>>>> attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal
>>>>>>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all
>>>>>>>>> browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find
>>>>>>>>> solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it
>>>>>>>>> and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> > > >
> > > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > > > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > > > > >
> > > > > > data() {
> > > > > > return {
> > > > > > showModal: false,
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > },
> > > > > >
> > > > > > mounted() {
> > > > > > this.showModal = true;
> > > > > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > > > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > },
> > > > > > watch: {
> > > > > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > > > > if (newValue) {
> > > > > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > > > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > },
> > > > > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > > > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > > > > },
> > > > > > methods: {
> > > > > > closeModal() {
> > > > > > this.showModal = false;
> > > > > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > > > > },
> > > > > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > > > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > > > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > > > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > > > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > > > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > > > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > } else {
> > > > > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > > > > event.preventDefault();
> > > > > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > }
> > > > > > }
> > > > > >
> > > > > > On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into it.
> > > > > >

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Shaun,
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
>
>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically
>> what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the
>> strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>> vue-project environment:
>>
>> data() {
>> return {
>> showModal: false,
>> }
>> },
>>
>> mounted() {
>> this.showModal = true;
>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>> }
>> },
>> watch: {
>> showModal(newValue) {
>> if (newValue) {
>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> } else {
>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> }
>> }
>> },
>> beforeDestroy() {
>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> },
>> methods: {
>> closeModal() {
>> this.showModal = false;
>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>> },
>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href],
>> input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length
>> - 1];
>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>> event.preventDefault();
>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>> }
>> } else {
>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>> event.preventDefault();
>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>> }
>> }
>> }
>> }
>> }
>>
>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>> into it.
>>
>> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 4:56 PM Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
>> wrote:
>>
>>> Thanks for the update, Eric!
>>>
>>> Regarding the focus trap, I’m sure you’ve consulted a lot of sources
>>> already. If it’s helpful and not overstepping, I had this article

>>>>> this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
>>>>> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
>>>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.

>>>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal,
>>>>> which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/

>>>>> to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a
>>>>> pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted
>>>>> users.
>>>>>
>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation
>>>>> and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance,
>>>>> there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the
>>>>> overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a
>>>>> tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be
>>>>> here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something
>>>>> but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is
>>>>> better than bad aria".
>>>>>
>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used
>>>>> as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>>>

>>>>> close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we

>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based
>>>>>>>> on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements
>>>>>>>> as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the
>>>>>>>> way it should.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when
>>>>>>>> using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes
>>>>>>>> that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal
>>>>>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers.
>>>>>>>> These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them
>>>>>>>> quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll
>>>>>>>> take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

On Mar 8, 2024 at 12:16 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> This is reference for our call...
> AR-07, 09 and others...
> <Screenshot 2024-03-08 at 5.51.10 AM.png><AR-07-implementation.png><Screenshot 2024-03-08 at 5.52.49 AM.png>
>
> > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
> > > No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test vue-project environment:
> > >
> > > data() {
> > > return {
> > > showModal: false,
> > > }
> > > },
> > >
> > > mounted() {
> > > this.showModal = true;
> > > document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
> > > if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
> > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > }
> > > },
> > > watch: {
> > > showModal(newValue) {
> > > if (newValue) {
> > > this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> > > document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > } else {
> > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > }
> > > }
> > > },
> > > beforeDestroy() {
> > > document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> > > },
> > > methods: {
> > > closeModal() {
> > > this.showModal = false;
> > > document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> > > },
> > > handleKeyDown(event) {
> > > const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> > > const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href], input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> > > const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1];
> > > if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> > > if (event.shiftKey) {
> > > if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> > > event.preventDefault();
> > > lastFocusableElement.focus();
> > > }
> > > } else {
> > > if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> > > event.preventDefault();
> > > focusableElements[0].focus();
> > > }
> > > }
> > > }
> > > }
> > > }
> > >
> > > On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into it.
> > >
> > > > On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 4:56 PM Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com> wrote:
> > > > > Thanks for the update, Eric!
> > > > >
> > > > > Regarding the focus trap, I’m sure you’ve consulted a lot of sources already. If it’s helpful and not overstepping, I had this article bookmarked for cases where I may need to implement this on other sites. I haven’t tested it, so maybe it’s garbage, but thought I’d share just in case.
> > > > >
> > > > > Concerning the focus indicator the more I’ve navigated around the site, the more I’ve realized that forcing a standard color in the code may prove problematic. As the focus shifts over white, green, blue, and orange backgrounds we may find we need to contextualize the styling. Please keep me updated on what you find as you begin working on that. I’d hate to see you spend too much time on it only to find we have to shift approaches, so let’s keep that one on a short leash for the time being.

> > > > > > > > > > > > Shaun,
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
> > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> On Fri, Mar 1, 2024 at 5:20 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:
>
>> No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically
>> what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the
>> strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
>> vue-project environment:
>>
>> data() {
>> return {
>> showModal: false,
>> }
>> },
>>
>> mounted() {
>> this.showModal = true;
>> document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
>> if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>> }
>> },
>> watch: {
>> showModal(newValue) {
>> if (newValue) {
>> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
>> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> } else {
>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> }
>> }
>> },
>> beforeDestroy() {
>> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
>> },
>> methods: {
>> closeModal() {
>> this.showModal = false;
>> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
>> },
>> handleKeyDown(event) {
>> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
>> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href],
>> input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
>> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length
>> - 1];
>> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
>> if (event.shiftKey) {
>> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
>> event.preventDefault();
>> lastFocusableElement.focus();
>> }
>> } else {
>> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
>> event.preventDefault();
>> focusableElements[0].focus();
>> }
>> }
>> }
>> }
>> }
>>
>> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time
>> into it.
>>

>>>>> users.

>>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation
>>>>> and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance,
>>>>> there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the
>>>>> overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a
>>>>> tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be
>>>>> here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something
>>>>> but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is
>>>>> better than bad aria".
>>>>>
>>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used
>>>>> as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based
>>>>>>>> on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements
>>>>>>>> as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the
>>>>>>>> way it should.
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when
>>>>>>>> using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes
>>>>>>>> that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal
>>>>>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere
>>>>>>>>    within the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>>>
>>>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers.
>>>>>>>> These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them
>>>>>>>> quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll
>>>>>>>> take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

> },
> watch: {
> showModal(newValue) {
> if (newValue) {
> this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
> document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> } else {
> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> }
> }
> },
> beforeDestroy() {
> document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
> },
> methods: {
> closeModal() {
> this.showModal = false;
> document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
> },
> handleKeyDown(event) {
> const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
> const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href],
> input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
> const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length -
> 1];
> if (event.key === 'Tab') {
> if (event.shiftKey) {
> if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
> event.preventDefault();
> lastFocusableElement.focus();
> }
> } else {
> if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
> event.preventDefault();
> focusableElements[0].focus();
> }
> }
> }
> }
> }
>
> On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into
> it.
>

>>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.

>>>> pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted

>>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation
>>>> and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance,
>>>> there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the
>>>> overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a
>>>> tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be
>>>> here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something
>>>> but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is
>>>> better than bad aria".
>>>>
>>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as
>>>> a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

==== EMAIL 521 ====
Date: Fri, 1 Mar 2024 17:20:36 -0700
From: Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
To:   Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
Subject: Re: Accordions

No worries - totally fine. Yeah this article is good and it's basically
what I've implemented but the Vue version rather than Javascript - the
strange thing is that the same setup works beautifully in my test
vue-project environment:

data() {
return {
showModal: false,
}
},

mounted() {
this.showModal = true;
document.body.classList.add('noscroll');
if (this.$refs.modalDialog) {
this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
}
},
watch: {
showModal(newValue) {
if (newValue) {
this.$refs.modalDialog.focus();
document.addEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
} else {
document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
}
}
},
beforeDestroy() {
document.removeEventListener('keydown', this.handleKeyDown);
},
methods: {
closeModal() {
this.showModal = false;
document.body.classList.remove('noscroll');
},
handleKeyDown(event) {
const modalDialog = this.$refs.modalDialog;
const focusableElements = modalDialog.querySelectorAll('button, [href],
input, select, textarea, [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])');
const lastFocusableElement = focusableElements[focusableElements.length - 1
];
if (event.key === 'Tab') {
if (event.shiftKey) {
if (document.activeElement === focusableElements[0]) {
event.preventDefault();
lastFocusableElement.focus();
}
} else {
if (document.activeElement === lastFocusableElement) {
event.preventDefault();
focusableElements[0].focus();
}
}
}
}
}

On the focus indicator - that makes sense to not invest too much time into
it.

>>> On Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>,
>>> wrote:
>>>
>>> Shaun,
>>>
>>> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this
>>> are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
>>> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
>>> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>>>
>>> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal,
>>> which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
>>> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>>>
>>> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted
>>> to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a
>>> pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted
>>> users.
>>>
>>> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and
>>> semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an
>>> aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that
>>> "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element
>>> to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested
>>> with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of
>>> it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad
>>> aria".
>>>
>>> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as
>>> a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the
>>> description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents
>>> a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a
>>> dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility
>>> that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For
>>> now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>>>
>>> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty
>>> close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we
>>> should be good.
>>>
>>> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.
>>>

>>>>>> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based
>>>>>> on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements
>>>>>> as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the
>>>>>> way it should.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when
>>>>>> using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes
>>>>>> that it should have "out of the box":
>>>>>>
>>>>>>    - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal
>>>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within
>>>>>>    the focus of the modal elements
>>>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>>>
>>>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers.
>>>>>> These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them
>>>>>> quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll
>>>>>> take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

Thanks for the update, Eric!

Regarding the focus trap, I’m sure you’ve consulted a lot of sources already. If it’s helpful and not overstepping, I had this article bookmarked for cases where I may need to implement this on other sites. I haven’t tested it, so maybe it’s garbage, but thought I’d share just in case.

Concerning the focus indicator the more I’ve navigated around the site, the more I’ve realized that forcing a standard color in the code may prove problematic. As the focus shifts over white, green, blue, and orange backgrounds we may find we need to contextualize the styling. Please keep me updated on what you find as you begin working on that. I’d hate to see you spend too much time on it only to find we have to shift approaches, so let’s keep that one on a short leash for the time being.

Thanks again,

> > > > > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.
> > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > On Fri, Feb 23, 2024 at 10:38 PM Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com> wrote:
> > > > > > > > > Thanks for the update, Eric. I hope you have a great weekend!

> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • First focus is close modal when open
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

Just a quick update: I fixed the issue where the outline was cut off on the
right within the modal, cleaned up the javascript that was interfering with
the focus trap - Mark and crew had set up a custom handling for the modal
which is now built into the dialog semantic element so I removed the
unnecessary parts.

The focus trap is not working - I've implemented 5 different ways to build
the focus trap and they all fail, so I'm tracking down why - and I have on
my list to standardize the focus outline across browsers. Once I get these
two sorted out for the home page I'll filter them down to the other modals
across the site.

> > > Taking a look at the modal, I noted a few things that may still need some adjustment:
> > >
> > > 1. When I load the page with Mac VoiceOver active, the modal receives focus automatically, which is perfect, but it states “Dialog with three items.” That count seems misleading to me. I’m not sure which three items it’s detecting though?
> > > 2. Looking at item AR-06 in the BeAccessible detailed spreadsheet, it sounds like we need to label the “More info” links so that they describe what readers can get more info about. I’m sure another alternative would be to remove the “More info” link altogether and make the text heading for each item clickable. Either is fine from an accessibility standpoint as far as I know, so I’ll leave it up to you which is easier to implement.
> > > 3. The new modal code seems to have broken the functionality of the modals in the other places they’re used (news items on the homepage, calendar modals, etc.). That’s fine in the dev environment, but we’ll need to make sure we don’t release that to production until it’s resolved.
> > >
> > > Regarding your suggestion of the focus indicator, where are you seeing the black outline? I’m mostly seeing blue on my end, but I wonder if that’s a system default not directly coded into the website. I’m fine with whatever you think, but we’ll need to make sure it’s visible in all contexts, so if we’re using a blue line to show focus on the close button within the blue heading bar of the modal that would obviously represent a contrast issue, so we may need to change it to white in that context and likely others.
> > >
> > > I wish I had better insight into the aria label you mentioned on the modal background, but I have no clue why it was coded that way. I’ll default to your expertise and opinion when in doubt on items like this, so feel free to take the reins and do what you think is best. Worst case, we need to go back and add it back in.
> > >
> > > Jumping over to the MailChimp modal, the BeAccessible audit has several lined related to that feature, so we may need to take a wide-angle view on what’s working, what’s not, and what we have control over. That code is mostly just copy and pasted as a plugin from their platform, so we may be limited in what we can control.
> > >
> > > Moving forward once you have the modals wrapped up, I’d like to focus in on completing as many of the items in the BeAccessible detailed spreadsheet as possible. Financially, I think this will make the most sense for Aspen. Since BeAccessible needs to confirm remediation on each line item in the spreadsheet, I’d like to get through as many of the simple/easy ones as possible before the deadline for free validation ends so that if we need them to verify anything after that date at their hourly rate, we’re having them look at as few items as possible.

> > > On Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> > > > Shaun,
> > > >
> > > > I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
> > > >
> > > > So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
> > > >
> > > > Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
> > > >
> > > > Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
> > > >
> > > > I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
> > > >
> > > > I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
> > > >
> > > > Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.
> > > >

1. I will check into this and see what I find.
2. The "more info" aria-label I have fixed, I just haven't pushed it to the
develop branch yet so you'll see that one either Friday or Monday.
3. Yes, the way they have the code set up is there's one file that holds
the main modal layout then 5 types of content that are dynamically rendered
according to the page, so that one is on my list for after the status
content is resolved.

For the focus indicator, this is a fair point with the browser question.
I'm using Firefox primarily, so it looks like this:
[image: Screenshot 2024-02-27 at 12.39.26 PM.png]
However, Safari is:
[image: Screenshot 2024-02-27 at 12.38.51 PM.png]
Chrome:
[image: Screenshot 2024-02-27 at 12.40.42 PM.png]
and Edge:
[image: Screenshot 2024-02-27 at 12.41.57 PM.png]
Firefox is the hardest to see and these are clearly all browser fallbacks,
not from the code so I'll just take a look at the CSS and make it uniform.
Either Chrome or Safari look clearest I think so I'll plan on using one of
those two except dashed, this way the dashes will be either white or blue
depending on the background  - and that's easy to switch if we need to.

For the MailChimp modal, I'll let you know what I find when I get in there
- the form fields tab through just fine so I suspect the reader probably is
good too for the needed aria-labels. I think it will just be the modal's
esc to close, focus trap and autofocus on the close button we'll need to
implement.

As far as the focus for after the modal is complete, this makes complete
sense - get as far and fast as possible. I definitely will be making a
point to do that.

> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this
> are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>

> which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/

> a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel
> or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
>
> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and
> semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an
> aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that
> "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element
> to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested
> with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of
> it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad
> aria".
>
> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a
> container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description
> for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or
> non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable
> alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that
> there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now,
> I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>
> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty
> close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we
> should be good.
>
> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

>>>> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when
>>>> using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes

>>>>

>>>>    should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
>>>>    - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within
>>>>    the focus of the modal elements
>>>>    - First focus is close modal when open
>>>>    - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>>>>
>>>> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers.
>>>> These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them
>>>> quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll
>>>> take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

==== EMAIL 526 ====
Date: Mon, 26 Feb 2024 17:59:22 -0700
From: Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
To:   Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
Subject: Re: Accordions

Hi Eric,

Thanks for the recap as always.

Taking a look at the modal, I noted a few things that may still need some adjustment:

1. When I load the page with Mac VoiceOver active, the modal receives focus automatically, which is perfect, but it states “Dialog with three items.” That count seems misleading to me. I’m not sure which three items it’s detecting though?
2. Looking at item AR-06 in the BeAccessible detailed spreadsheet, it sounds like we need to label the “More info” links so that they describe what readers can get more info about. I’m sure another alternative would be to remove the “More info” link altogether and make the text heading for each item clickable. Either is fine from an accessibility standpoint as far as I know, so I’ll leave it up to you which is easier to implement.
3. The new modal code seems to have broken the functionality of the modals in the other places they’re used (news items on the homepage, calendar modals, etc.). That’s fine in the dev environment, but we’ll need to make sure we don’t release that to production until it’s resolved.

Regarding your suggestion of the focus indicator, where are you seeing the black outline? I’m mostly seeing blue on my end, but I wonder if that’s a system default not directly coded into the website. I’m fine with whatever you think, but we’ll need to make sure it’s visible in all contexts, so if we’re using a blue line to show focus on the close button within the blue heading bar of the modal that would obviously represent a contrast issue, so we may need to change it to white in that context and likely others.

I wish I had better insight into the aria label you mentioned on the modal background, but I have no clue why it was coded that way. I’ll default to your expertise and opinion when in doubt on items like this, so feel free to take the reins and do what you think is best. Worst case, we need to go back and add it back in.

Jumping over to the MailChimp modal, the BeAccessible audit has several lined related to that feature, so we may need to take a wide-angle view on what’s working, what’s not, and what we have control over. That code is mostly just copy and pasted as a plugin from their platform, so we may be limited in what we can control.

Moving forward once you have the modals wrapped up, I’d like to focus in on completing as many of the items in the BeAccessible detailed spreadsheet as possible. Financially, I think this will make the most sense for Aspen. Since BeAccessible needs to confirm remediation on each line item in the spreadsheet, I’d like to get through as many of the simple/easy ones as possible before the deadline for free validation ends so that if we need them to verify anything after that date at their hourly rate, we’re having them look at as few items as possible.

Let me know if you have any questions or need anything from my end.

Shaun Cooney } MONUMENT GRAPHICS | DESIGN + WEB
PO Box 299, Fruita, CO 81521
p* 970/242-4311 • MonumentGraphics.com
On Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> Shaun,
>
> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>
> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which can be seen on the Servd develop page: https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>
> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
>
> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad aria".
>
> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now, I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>
> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we should be good.
>
> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.
>

> > > > > > > > > > • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
> > > > > > > > > >
> > > > > > > > > > The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.

On Mon, Feb 26, 2024 at 4:51 PM Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com> wrote:

> Shaun,
>
> I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this
> are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
> using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
> to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.
>
> So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal,
> which can be seen on the Servd develop page:
> https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/
>
> Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to
> a blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel
> or maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.
>
> Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and
> semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an
> aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that
> "fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element
> to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested
> with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of
> it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad
> aria".
>
> I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a
> container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description
> for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or
> non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable
> alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that
> there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now,
> I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.
>
> I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty
> close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we
> should be good.
>
> Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

==== EMAIL 528 ====
Date: Mon, 26 Feb 2024 16:51:27 -0700
From: Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
To:   Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
Subject: Re: Accordions

Shaun,

I've been working on the modal elements all day today - the docs on this
are a bit murkier than for an accessible accordion; for instance, when
using the semantic element for a modal (<dialog>), it's required to use JS
to open/close properly and does not focus trap without additional coding.

So far, I have sorted out everything but the focus trap on the modal, which
can be seen on the Servd develop page:
https://tense-deer-development.cl-us-east-1.servd.dev/

Also, though, I think the outline for the tab/focus should be adjusted to a
blue outline instead of black and bump up the thickness by half a pixel or
maybe a full pixel to make the tab focus more clear to sighted users.

Just to note, I'm a bit confused by some of the aria- implementation and
semantic elements that have previously been coded. For instance, there's an
aria-hidden=true on the div that contains the styling for the overlay that
"fades" the background behind the modal, but this isn't a tabbable element
to begin with so I can't find any reason for it to be here. I've tested
with and without out just in case I'm missing something but at the end of
it I've just removed it since the W3C rule is "no aria is better than bad
aria".

I also noted that there's a dialog element in the footer that's used as a
container for the newsletter subscription, which seems odd; the description
for a <dialog> element from MDN is an element that "represents a modal or
non-modal dialog box or other interactive component, such as a dismissable
alert, inspector, or subwindow" - so there's a slim possibility that
there's a precedent for this usage but it remains questionable. For now,
I'm just noting it for when the footer is worked on next.

I was hoping the modal wouldn't be quite so difficult but we're pretty
close now, I just need to trap the focus when the modal is open and we
should be good.

Let me know if you see anything else needing adjusted with it.

On Feb 23, 2024 at 7:35 AM -0700, Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>, wrote:
> For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into production or do you want me to do that part?
>
> For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it should.
>
> First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it should have "out of the box":
>
> • Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
> • Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the focus of the modal elements
> • First focus is close modal when open
> • Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable
>
> The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.
>

==== EMAIL 534 ====
Date: Fri, 23 Feb 2024 07:34:58 -0700
From: Eric Phifer <eric@ericphiferllc.com>
To:   Shaun Cooney <shaun@monumentgraphics.com>
Subject: Re: Accordions

For the accordion, do you usually take these kinds of updates into
production or do you want me to do that part?

For the modal, I'm working with a simple version of the modal based on the
W3C specifications - as you know, I prefer to use semantic elements as they
tend to work better all around. In this case, it's not working the way it
should.

First, the modal box becomes focusable, which is not a problem when using
the <div role="dialog"> method. Second, there are a few attributes that it
should have "out of the box":

   - Closed loop when modal is open: everything behind the modal should be
   inaccessible by screen readers and keyboard functionality
   - Close modal with esc: this should be available anywhere within the
   focus of the modal elements
   - First focus is close modal when open
   - Anchor elements within the modal are tabbable

The last two succeed with both, the first two fail on all browsers. These
are the issues I'm working on today; if I can find solutions for them
quickly I'll have the modal in develop today, test it and once cleared I'll
take it to the sandbox for BeAccessible testing.
